\Gamma_{i},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n},\psi[F_{\Pi}/\Pi]\vdash F_{Q}[\bar{s}],\Delta_{i}
correspond to the definitional rules
\forall\bar{y}:Q(\bar{s})\leftarrow\psi
in
\Phi_{i}
, where
\Phi_{i}
is the definition defining
P
.
28
28

28

Since there are no negative occurrences of defined predicates in
\psi
, we can simplify
\psi[F_{\Pi}/\Pi^{+}]
to
\psi[F_{\Pi}/\Pi]
.

It shows that the major premise
\Gamma_{i},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n},F_{P}[\bar{t}]\vdash\Delta_{i}
or one of the minor premises is not cut-free provable, for otherwise,
\Gamma_{i},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\Delta_{i}
would be cut-free provable.
In the first case, we pick
\Gamma_{i+1}\coloneq\Gamma_{i}\cup\{F_{P}[\bar{t}]\}
and
\Delta_{i+1}\coloneq\Delta_{i}
.
In the second case, we choose a minor premise
\Gamma_{i},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n},\psi[F_{\Pi}/\Pi]\vdash F_{Q}[\bar{s}],\Delta_{i}
that is not cut-free provable (any choice works), and pick
\Gamma_{i+1}\coloneq\Gamma_{i}\cup\{\psi[F_{\Pi}/\Pi]\}
and
\Delta_{i+1}\coloneq\Delta_{i}\cup\{F_{Q}[\bar{s}]\}
.

Note that, by construction, we ensured that for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
:

•

\Gamma_{i}
and
\Delta_{i}
are finite;

•

\Gamma_{i}\subseteq\Gamma_{i+1}
and
\Delta_{i}\subseteq\Delta_{i+1}
;

•

the sequent
\Gamma_{i},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\Delta_{i}
is not cut-free provable.

Finally, we let
\Gamma_{\omega}\coloneq\cup_{i=0}^{\infty}\Gamma_{i}
and
\Delta_{\omega}\coloneq\cup_{i=0}^{\infty}\Delta_{i}
, and we call
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\Delta_{\omega}
the
limit sequent
.
This sequent is not cut-free provable.
Indeed, if it were, then there would exist finite sets
\Gamma^{\prime}\subseteq\Gamma\cup\{\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\}
and
\Delta^{\prime}\subseteq\Delta
such that
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\Delta^{\prime}
is cut-free provable.
This follows by a straightforward argument by induction on the height of a cut-free proof of
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\Delta_{\omega}
.
However, since
\Gamma^{\prime}\subseteq\Gamma_{i}\cup\{\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\}
and
\Delta^{\prime}\subseteq\Delta_{i}
for some
i\in\mathbb{N}
, this would entail that
\Gamma_{i},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\Delta_{i}
is cut-free provable, which is a contradiction.

We proceed by constructing the
Henkin counter-model

(\mathcal{I}_{\omega},\mathcal{H}_{\omega})
for
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\Delta_{\omega}
.
The domain of
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}
consists of all equivalence classes
[t]
of terms
t\in\textrm{Terms}(\Sigma)
under an equivalence relation
\sim
over
\textrm{Terms}(\Sigma)
:

Definition D.2.

We define a binary relation
\sim
on
\textrm{Terms}(\Sigma)
by the rules:

(i)

if
t=s\in\Gamma_{\omega}
, then
t\sim s
;

(ii)

t\sim t
;

(iii)

if
t\sim s
, then
s\sim t
;

(iv)

if
t\sim s
and
s\sim u
, then
t\sim u
;

(v)

if
t_{1}\sim s_{1},\dots,t_{k}\sim s_{k}
and
f
is a
k
-ary function symbol, then
f(t_{1},\dots,t_{k})\sim f(s_{1},\dots,s_{k})
.

By
(ii)
-
(iv)
,
\sim
is an equivalence relation.
We denote the equivalence class of a term
t\in\textrm{Terms}(\Sigma)
under
\sim
by
[t]
, and the set of all equivalence classes under
\sim
by
\textrm{Terms}(\Sigma)/\sim
.
We will sometimes write
(t_{1},\dots,t_{k})\sim(s_{1},\dots,s_{k})
for
t_{1}\sim s_{1},\dots,t_{k}\sim s_{k}
,
and
[(t_{1},\dots,t_{k})]
for
([t_{1}],\dots,[t_{k}])
.

We construct
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}
as the last structure
\mathcal{I}_{n}
of a finite sequence of structures
\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{I}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{I}_{n}
.
In defining this sequence, we use the operators
\mathcal{C}_{\Phi_{i}}
, for
i\in\{1,\dots,n\}
, as defined in Section
5.1
.
Since the definitions
\Phi_{i}
are positive,
\mathcal{C}_{\Phi}
depends only on the first argument.
Hence, by abuse of notation, we simply write
\mathcal{C}_{\Phi_{i}}(\mathcal{I})
instead of
\mathcal{C}_{\Phi_{i}}(\mathcal{I},\mathcal{J})
.

Definition D.3.

We define a sequence of FO
\xspace
-structures
\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{I}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{I}_{n}
by induction on
i
:

First, we define
\mathcal{I}_{0}
as the FO
\xspace
-structure with
\mathsf{voc}(\mathcal{I}_{0})=\Sigma\setminus\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}\mathrm{def}(\Phi_{i})
and
\mathsf{dom}(\mathcal{I}_{0})=\textrm{Terms}(\Sigma)/\sim
, such that:

•

for every
k
-ary function symbol
f
in
\Sigma
:
f^{\mathcal{I}_{0}}([t_{1}],\dots,[t_{k}])=[f^{\mathcal{I}_{0}}(t_{1},\dots,t_{k})]
for all
t_{1},\dots,t_{k}\in\textrm{Terms}(\Sigma)
;

•

for every
k
-ary predicate symbol
P
in
\Sigma
:
P^{\mathcal{I}_{0}}=\{([t_{1}],\dots,[t_{k}])\mid P(t_{1},\dots,t_{k})\in\Gamma_{\omega}\}
.

Note that
f^{\mathcal{I}_{0}}
is well-defined by rule
(v)
of Definition
D.2
.

Suppose that we have defined the structure
\mathcal{I}_{i-1}
for some
i\in\{1,\dots,n\}
.
Since
\Gamma,\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\Delta
is a canonical sequent, no parameter is a defined predicate of
\Phi_{j}
for any
j>i
.
Hence, every parameter of
\Phi_{i}
is in
\mathsf{voc}(\mathcal{I}_{i-1})=\Sigma\setminus\bigcup_{j=i}^{n}\mathrm{def}(\Phi_{j})
.
Therefore,
\mathcal{O}_{i}\coloneq\mathcal{I}_{i-1}|_{\mathrm{pars}(\Phi_{i})}
is a
\Phi_{i}
-context.
Let
\mathcal{J}_{i}
be the least
\mathrm{sym}(\Phi_{i})
-structure expanding
\mathcal{O}_{i}
such that:

(i)

for all
P\in\mathrm{def}(\Phi_{i})
: if
P(\bar{t})\in\Gamma_{\omega}
, then
[\bar{t}]\in P^{\mathcal{J}_{i}}
;

(ii)

\mathcal{C}_{\Phi_{i}}(\mathcal{J}_{i})\sqsubseteq\mathcal{J}_{i}
.

The existence of
\mathcal{J}_{i}
follows from the Knaster-Tarski theorem
(
Tarski 1955
)
.
We define
\mathcal{I}_{i}
as the expansion of
\mathcal{I}_{i-1}
with
\mathsf{voc}(\mathcal{I}_{i})=\mathsf{voc}(\mathcal{I}_{i-1})\cup\mathrm{def}(\Phi_{i})
, such that
P^{\mathcal{I}_{i}}=P^{\mathcal{J}_{i}}
for all
P\in\mathrm{def}(\Phi_{i})
.

In sum, we have defined a sequence of structures
\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{I}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{I}_{n}
.
We define
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}
as
\mathcal{I}_{n}
.

Proposition D.4.

t^{\mathcal{I}_{\omega}}=[t]
for all
t\in\textrm{Terms}(\Sigma)
.

Proof.

We show that
t^{\mathcal{I}_{\omega}}=[t]
for all
t\in\textrm{Terms}(\Sigma)
, by structural induction on
t
:
29
29

29

Note that we do not need a separate case for object symbols, as we defined them to be
0
-ary function symbols.

Case
:
t
is of the form
f(t_{1},\dots,t_{k})
for a
k
-ary function symbol
f
and terms
t_{1},\dots,t_{k}
.
By induction hypothesis,
t_{i}^{\mathcal{I}_{\omega}}=[t_{i}]
for each
i\in\{1,\dots,k\}
.
By definition of
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}
,
f(t_{1},\dots,t_{k})^{\mathcal{I}_{\omega}}=f^{\mathcal{I}_{\omega}}(t_{1}^{\mathcal{I}_{\omega}},\dots,t_{k}^{\mathcal{I}_{\omega}})=f^{\mathcal{I}_{\omega}}([t_{1}],\dots,[t_{k}])=[f(t_{1},\dots,t_{k})]
.
∎

Lemma D.5.

Let
x
be an object symbol and
t
and
s
terms.
If
t\sim s
, then
\Gamma_{\omega}\vdash\varphi[t/x]
is cut-free provable iff
\Gamma_{\omega}\vdash\varphi[s/x]
is cut-free provable for every
FO
\xspace
-formula
\varphi
.

Proof.

Let
t
and
s
be terms such that
t\sim s
.
Then there exists a tree-shaped derivation
T
for the fact that
t\sim s
, according to the rules
(i)
-
(v)
of Definition
D.2
.
We show by induction on the height of
T
that, for every FO
\xspace
-formula
\varphi
and object symbol
x
,
\Gamma_{\omega}\vdash\varphi[t/x]
is cut-free provable iff
\Gamma_{\omega}\vdash\varphi[s/x]
is cut-free provable:

Case
:
t\sim s
is derived by
(i)
.
Then
t=s\in\Gamma_{\omega}
.
By application of (=L), a cut-free proof of
\Gamma_{\omega}\vdash\varphi[t/x]
can be extended to a cut-free proof of
\Gamma_{\omega}\vdash\varphi[s/x]
and vice versa.

Case
:
t\sim s
is derived by
(ii)
.
Then
s\doteq t
.
Trivially,
\Gamma_{\omega}\vdash\varphi[t/x]
is cut-free provable iff
\Gamma_{\omega}\vdash\varphi[t/x]
is cut-free provable.

Case
:
t\sim s
is derived by
(iii)
from
s\sim t
.
By induction hypothesis,
\Gamma_{\omega}\vdash\varphi[s/x]
is cut-free provable iff
\Gamma_{\omega}\vdash\varphi[t/x]
is cut-free provable.
Then, of course,
\Gamma_{\omega}\vdash\varphi[t/x]
is cut-free provable iff
\Gamma_{\omega}\vdash\varphi[s/x]
is cut-free provable.

Case
:
t\sim s
is derived by
(iv)
from
t\sim u
and
u\sim s
for a term
u
.
By induction hypothesis,
\Gamma_{\omega}\vdash\varphi[t/x]
is cut-free provable iff
\Gamma_{\omega}\vdash\varphi[u/x]
is cut-free provable, and
\Gamma_{\omega}\vdash\varphi[u/x]
is cut-free provable iff
\Gamma_{\omega}\vdash\varphi[s/x]
is cut-free provable.
Then
\Gamma_{\omega}\vdash\varphi[t/x]
is cut-free provable
iff
\Gamma_{\omega}\vdash\varphi[s/x]
is cut-free provable.

Case
:
t\sim s
is derived by
(v)
from
t_{1}\sim s_{1}
, …,
t_{k},\sim s_{k}
for terms
t_{1},\dots,t_{k},s_{1},\dots,s_{k}
.
Then
t\doteq f(t_{1},\dots,t_{k})
and
t\doteq f(s_{1},\dots,s_{k})
.
By induction hypothesis, we know that for every
i\in\{1,\dots,k\}
and every FO
\xspace
-formula
\varphi
, the sequent
\Gamma_{\omega}\vdash\varphi[t_{i}/x]
is cut-free provable iff
\Gamma_{\omega}\vdash\varphi[s_{i}/x]
is cut-free provable.
We derive that

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\omega}\vdash\varphi[f(t_{1},\dots,t_{k})/x]\text{ is cut-free provable}

iff

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\omega}\vdash\varphi[f(s_{1},t_{2}\dots,t_{k})/x]\text{ is cut-free provable}

iff

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\omega}\vdash\varphi[f(s_{1},\dots,s_{k})/x]\text{ is cut-free provable.}

∎

Lemma D.6.

Let
P
be a defined predicate of one of the definitions
\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}
.
If
[\bar{t}]\in P^{\mathcal{I}_{\omega}}
, then
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash P(\bar{t})
is cut-free provable.

Proof.

We prove by induction on
i
that, for every
i\in\{1,\dots,n\}
and every
P\in\mathrm{def}(\Phi_{i})
, if
[\bar{t}]\in P^{\mathcal{I}_{\omega}}
, then
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash P(\bar{t})
is cut-free provable.

For each
P\in\mathrm{def}(\Phi_{i})
, we denote by
X_{P}
the set
\{[\bar{t}]\mid\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash P(\bar{t})\text{ is cut-free provable}\}
.

Note that if
[\bar{t}]\in X_{P}
for some
P\in\mathrm{def}(\Phi)
, then there exists a tuple of terms
\bar{s}
such that
\bar{s}\sim\bar{t}
and
\Gamma_{\omega}\vdash P(\bar{s})
is cut-free provable.
By repeated application of Lemma
D.5
, it follows that
\Gamma_{\omega}\vdash P(\bar{t})
is cut-free provable as well.
We will use this fact multiple times in the remainder of the proof.

Let
\mathcal{J}\coloneq\mathcal{I}_{\omega}|_{\mathrm{pars}(\Phi_{i})}[\mathrm{def}(\Phi_{i}):X_{\mathrm{def}(\Phi_{i})}]
.
In words,
\mathcal{J}
is the restriction of
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}
to the parameters of
\Phi_{i}
, expanded to interpret the defined predicates
P
of
\Phi
by the sets
X_{P}
.
We will show that
\mathcal{J}
satisfies:

(i)

for all
P\in\mathrm{def}(\Phi_{i})
: if
P(\bar{t})\in\Gamma_{\omega}
, then
[\bar{t}]\in P^{\mathcal{J}}
;

(ii)

\mathcal{C}_{\Phi_{i}}(\mathcal{J})\sqsubseteq\mathcal{J}
.

Since, by Definition
D.3
,
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}|_{\mathrm{sym}(\Phi_{i})}
is the least such structure, this will entail that
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}|_{\mathrm{sym}(\Phi_{i})}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{J}
, and hence that

P^{\mathcal{I}_{\omega}}=P^{\mathcal{I}_{\omega}|_{\mathrm{sym}(\Phi_{i})}}\subseteq P^{\mathcal{J}}=X_{P}

for all
P\in\mathrm{def}(\Phi)
.
By definition of
X_{P}
, this means that if
[\bar{t}]\in P^{\mathcal{I}_{\omega}}
, then
\Gamma_{\omega}\vdash P(\bar{t})
is cut-free provable, thereby finishing the proof.

For
(i)
, let
P\in\mathrm{def}(\Phi_{i})
, and assume that
P(\bar{t})\in\Gamma_{\omega}
.
Then
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash P(\bar{t})
is cut-free provable via (ax).
It thus remains to show
(ii)
, i.e., that
\mathcal{C}_{\Phi_{i}}(\mathcal{J})\sqsubseteq\mathcal{J}
.
Let
P\in\mathrm{def}(\Phi_{i})
.
We need to show that
P^{\mathcal{C}_{\Phi_{i}}(\mathcal{J})}\subseteq P^{\mathcal{J}}
.
Take
[\bar{t}]\in P^{\mathcal{C}_{\Phi_{i}}(\mathcal{J})}
.
By definition of
\mathcal{C}_{\Phi_{i}}
, this means that
\mathcal{J}[\bar{y}:[\bar{t}]]\models\varphi_{P}(\bar{y})
, where
\bar{y}
is an
\mathsf{ar}(P)
-tuple of object symbols not occurring freely in the body
\varphi
of any definitional rule of
\Phi
of the form
\forall\bar{x}:P(\bar{t})\leftarrow\varphi
.
Since
\bar{t}^{\mathcal{J}}=\bar{t}^{\mathcal{I}_{\omega}}=[\bar{t}]
by Proposition
D.4
, this implies
\mathcal{J}\models\varphi_{P}(\bar{t})
.
We claim that
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\varphi_{P}(\bar{t})
is cut-free provable.
Via (def R), this claim entails that
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash P(\bar{t})
is cut-free provable, and hence, that
[\bar{t}]\in X_{P}=P^{\mathcal{J}}
, finishing the proof.
We prove the claim by showing that if
\mathcal{J}\models\varphi_{P}(\bar{t})
, then
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\varphi_{P}(\bar{t})
is cut-free provable.
We do so by structural induction on
\varphi_{P}(\bar{t})
:

Case
:
\varphi_{P}(\bar{t})
is of the form
t=s
.
Then
t\sim s
.
Via (
=
R),
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash t=t
is cut-free provable.
By Lemma
D.5
,
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash t=s
is cut-free provable as well.

Case
:
\varphi_{P}(\bar{t})
is of the form
Q(\bar{s})
.
The predicate symbol
Q
is either defined by
\Phi_{i}
, by
\Phi_{j}
for some
j<i
, or not defined by any
\Phi_{j}
for
j\in\{1,\dots,n\}
.
(Note that, since
\Gamma,\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\Delta
is a canonical sequent,
Q
cannot be defined by
\Phi_{j}
for any
j>i
.)

Subcase
:
Q
is defined by
\Phi_{i}
.
Since
\mathcal{J}\models Q(\bar{s})
, we have that
[\bar{s}]\in X_{Q}
, which implies that
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash Q(\bar{s})
by the above observation.

Subcase
:
Q
is defined by
\Phi_{j}
for some
j<i
.
Since
\mathcal{J}
and
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}
coincide on
Q
, we have that
[\bar{s}]\in Q^{\mathcal{I}_{\omega}}
.
By (the first) induction hypothesis,
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash Q(\bar{s})
is cut-free provable.

Subcase
:
Q
is not defined by any
\Phi_{j}
for
j\in\{1,\dots,n\}
.
Since
\mathcal{J}
and
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}
coincide on
Q
, we have that
[\bar{s}]\in Q^{\mathcal{I}_{\omega}}
.
By definition of
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}
, there exists a tuple of terms
\bar{u}
such that
\bar{u}\sim\bar{s}
and
Q(\bar{u})\in\Gamma_{\omega}
.
With a single application of (ax),
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash Q(\bar{u})
is cut-free provable
By repeated application of Lemma
D.5
, it follows that
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash Q(\bar{s})
is cut-free provable as well.

Case
:
\varphi_{P}(\bar{t})
is of the form
\varphi_{1}\land\varphi_{2}
.
Then
\mathcal{J}\models\varphi_{1}
and
\mathcal{J}\models\varphi_{2}
.
By (the second) induction hypothesis,
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\varphi_{1}
and
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\varphi_{2}
are cut-free provable, and through application of (
\land
R), so is
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\varphi_{1}\land\varphi_{2}
.

Case
:
\varphi_{P}(\bar{t})
is of the form
\varphi_{1}\lor\varphi_{2}
.
Then
\mathcal{J}\models\varphi_{1}
or
\mathcal{J}\models\varphi_{2}
.
By (the second) induction hypothesis,
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\varphi_{1}
or
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\varphi_{2}
is cut-free provable, and through application of (wk) and (
\lor
R), so is
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\varphi_{1}\lor\varphi_{2}
.
∎

Lemma D.7.

\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\models\varphi
for all
\varphi\in\Gamma_{\omega}
and
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\not\models\varphi
for all
\varphi\in\Delta_{\omega}
.

Proof.

We prove by structural induction on
\varphi
that if
\varphi\in\Gamma_{\omega}
, then
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\models\varphi
, and if
\varphi\in\Delta_{\omega}
, then
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\not\models\varphi
for all FO
\xspace
-formulas
\varphi
.

Case
:
\varphi
is of the form
t=s
.
If
t=s\in\Gamma_{\omega}
, then
t\sim s
by rule
(i)
of Definition
D.2
.
Consequently,
t^{\mathcal{I}_{\omega}}=[t]=[s]=s^{\mathcal{I}_{\omega}}
, and hence
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\models t=s
.

Now assume that
\varphi\in\Delta_{\omega}
and suppose for contradiction that
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\models t=s
.
Then
[t]=t^{\mathcal{I}_{\omega}}=s^{\mathcal{I}_{\omega}}=[s]
, whence
t\sim s
.
Since
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash t=t
is obviously cut-free provable, so is
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash t=s
, by Lemma
D.5
.
However, this implies that
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\Delta_{\omega}
is cut-free provable, which is a contradiction.
Thus,
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\not\models t=s
.

Case
:
\varphi
is of the form
P(\bar{t})
.
If
P(\bar{t})\in\Gamma_{\omega}
, then
[\bar{t}]\in P^{\mathcal{I}_{\omega}}
by definition of
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}
, whence
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\models P(\bar{t})
.

Now assume that
P(\bar{t})\in\Delta_{\omega}
, and suppose for contradiction that
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\models P(\bar{t})
.
Then
[\bar{t}]\in P^{\mathcal{I}_{\omega}}
.
Consequently,
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash P(\bar{t})
is cut-free provable.
Indeed, if
P
is defined by one of the definitions
\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}
, this follows from Lemma
D.6
.
Otherwise, there exists a tuple of terms
\bar{s}
such that
\bar{s}\sim\bar{t}
such that
P(\bar{s})\in\Gamma_{\omega}
, by definition of
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}
.
Through (ax),
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash P(\bar{s})
is cut-free provable, and by repeated application of Lemma
D.5
, so is
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash P(\bar{t})
.
Thus, in either case,
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash P(\bar{t})
is cut-free provable.
However, since
P(\bar{t})\in\Delta_{\omega}
, we obtain that
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\Delta_{\omega}
is cut-free provable, which is a contradiction.

Case
:
\varphi
is of the form
\lnot\psi
.
If
\lnot\psi\in\Gamma_{\omega}
, then
\psi\in\Delta_{\omega}
by construction of
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\Delta_{\omega}
.
Indeed, suppose that
\lnot\psi\in\Gamma_{\omega}
.
Then
\lnot\psi\in\Gamma_{j}
for some
j\in\mathbb{N}
.
Since
\lnot\psi
occurs infinitely often on the schedule
(e_{i})_{i}
, it occurs as the
i
-th schedule element
e_{i}
for some
i>j
.
By construction of
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\Delta_{\omega}
, the set
\Delta_{j+1}
contains
\psi
, and hence, so does
\Delta_{\omega}
.
By induction hypothesis,
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\not\models\psi
.
Consequently,
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\models\lnot\psi
.

If
\lnot\psi\in\Delta_{\omega}
, then
\psi\in\Gamma_{\omega}
by a similar reasoning as before.
By induction hypothesis,
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\models\psi
.
Hence,
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\not\models\lnot\psi
.

Case
:
\varphi
is of the form
\psi\land\chi
.
If
\psi\land\chi\in\Gamma_{\omega}
, then
\psi,\chi\in\Gamma_{\omega}
by construction of
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\Delta_{\omega}
.
By induction hypothesis,
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\models\psi
and
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\models\chi
.
Hence,
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\models\psi\land\chi
.

If
\psi\land\chi\in\Delta_{\omega}
, then
\psi\in\Delta_{\omega}
or
\chi\in\Delta_{\omega}
by construction of
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\Delta_{\omega}
.
By induction hypothesis,
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\not\models\psi
or
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\not\models\chi
.
Hence,
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\not\models\psi\land\chi
.

Cases
:
\varphi
is of the form
\psi\lor\chi
,
\psi\Rightarrow\chi
or
\psi\Leftrightarrow\chi
.
These cases are similar to the previous case.

Case
:
\varphi
is of the form
\forall x:\psi
.
If
\forall x:\psi\in\Gamma_{\omega}
, then
\psi[t/x]\in\Gamma_{\omega}
for any term
t
, by construction of
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\Delta_{\omega}
.
Indeed, suppose that
\forall x:\psi\in\Gamma_{\omega}
.
Then
\forall x:\psi\in\Gamma_{j}
for some
j\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
t
be a term.
Since
\langle\forall x:\psi,t\rangle
occurs infinitely often on the schedule
(e_{i})_{i}
, it occurs as the
i
-th schedule element of
(e_{i})_{i}
for some
i>j
.
By construction of
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\Delta_{\omega}
, the set
\Gamma_{i+1}
contains
\psi[t/x]
.
Hence,
\psi[t/x]\in\Gamma_{\omega}
.
By induction hypothesis,
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\models\psi[t/x]
.
Since
t^{\mathcal{I}_{\omega}}=[t]
by Proposition
D.4
, this implies
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}[x:[t]]\not\models\psi
.
Since any domain element of
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}
is of the form
[t]
for some term
t
, this shows that
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\models\forall x:\psi
.

If
\forall x:\psi\in\Delta_{\omega}
, then, by construction of
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\Delta_{\omega}
, the set
\Delta_{\omega}
contains
\psi[z/x]
for some object symbol
z
.
By induction hypothesis,
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\not\models\psi[z/x]
.
Since
z^{\mathcal{I}_{\omega}}=[z]
by definition of
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}
, this implies
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}[x:[z]]\not\models\psi
.
Consequently,
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\not\models\forall x:\psi
.

Case
:
\varphi
is of the form
\exists x:\psi
.
This case is similar to the previous case.
∎

Definition D.8.

The
Henkin counter-class

\mathcal{H}_{\omega}=\{H_{k}\mid k\in\mathbb{N}\}
is defined by

H_{k}=\{\{[\bar{t}]\in\mathsf{dom}(\mathcal{I}_{\omega})^{k}\mid\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\models\varphi[\bar{t}/\bar{x}]\}\mid\varphi\text{ is an {{FO}}\xspace-formula, $\bar{x}$ is a $k$-tuple of distinct object symbols}\}

for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
.

Lemma D.9.

The Henkin counter-class
\mathcal{H}_{\omega}
is a Henkin class for
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}
.

Proof.

Let
\{H_{k}^{\prime}\mid k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be the Henkin class of FO
\xspace
-definable sets for
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}
from Proposition
5.4
.
We show that the Henkin counter-class
\mathcal{H}_{\omega}
is equal to the class of FO
\xspace
-definable sets for
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}
, i.e., that
H_{k}=H_{k}^{\prime}
for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
The result then follows from Proposition
5.4
.

To show that
H_{k}\subseteq H_{k}^{\prime}
, pick a set
R\coloneq\{[\bar{t}]\in\mathsf{dom}(\mathcal{I}_{\omega})^{k}\mid\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\models\varphi[\bar{t}/\bar{x}]\}
from
H_{k}
.
By Proposition
D.4
,
R=\{([\bar{t}]\in\mathsf{dom}(\mathcal{I}_{\omega})^{k}\mid\mathcal{I}_{\omega}[\bar{x}:[\bar{t}]]\models\varphi\}
, and since each element of
\mathsf{dom}(\mathcal{I}_{\omega})
is of the form
[t]
,
R\in H_{k}^{\prime}
.

To show that
H_{k}^{\prime}\subseteq H_{k}
, pick a set
R\coloneq\{\bar{a}\in\mathsf{dom}(\mathcal{I}_{\omega})^{k}\mid\mathcal{I}[\bar{x}:\bar{a},\,\bar{y}:\bar{b}]\models\varphi\}
from
H_{k}^{\prime}
.
Since
\bar{b}
is a tuple over
\mathsf{dom}(\mathcal{I}_{\omega})
, it is of the form
[\bar{s}]
for a tuple of terms
\bar{s}
.
Hence, we can rewrite
R=\{[\bar{t}]\in\mathsf{dom}(\mathcal{I}_{\omega})^{k}\mid\mathcal{I}[\bar{x}:[\bar{t}],\,\bar{y}:[\bar{s}]]\models\varphi\}
.
By Proposition
D.4
,
R=\{[\bar{t}]\in\mathsf{dom}(\mathcal{I}_{\omega})^{k}\mid\mathcal{I}\models\varphi[\bar{s}/\bar{y},\bar{t}/\bar{x}]\}\in H_{k}
.
∎

Lemma D.10.

(\mathcal{I}_{\omega},\mathcal{H}_{\omega})\models_{\mathrm{H}}\Phi_{i}
for all
i\in\{1,\dots,n\}
.

Proof.

Take
i\in\{1,\dots,n\}
.
We need to show that
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}|_{\mathrm{sym}(\Phi_{i})}
is the least pre-fixed
\mathcal{H}_{\omega}
-point of
\mathcal{C}_{\Phi_{i}}
expanding
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}|_{\mathrm{pars}(\Phi_{i})}
.
Let
\mathcal{H}_{\omega}=\{H_{k}\mid k\in\mathbb{N}\}
.
First,
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}|_{\mathrm{sym}(\Phi_{i})}
is an
\mathcal{H}_{\omega}
-point.
This is because for every
P\in\mathrm{def}(\Phi_{i})
, we can write

P^{\mathcal{I}_{\omega}|_{\mathrm{sym}(\Phi_{i})}}
as
\{[\bar{t}]\in\mathsf{dom}(\mathcal{I}_{\omega})\mid\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\models P(\bar{t})\}
,
and since
P(\bar{t})
is an FO
\xspace
-formula,
P^{\mathcal{I}_{\omega}|_{\mathrm{sym}(\Phi_{i})}}\in H_{\mathsf{ar}(P)}
.
Furthermore,
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}|_{\mathrm{sym}(\Phi_{i})}
is a pre-fixpoint of
\mathcal{C}_{\Phi_{i}}
by definition of
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}
.
It thus remains to show that
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}|_{\mathrm{sym}(\Phi_{i})}
is the
least
pre-fixed
\mathcal{H}_{\omega}
-point of
\mathcal{C}_{\Phi_{i}}
expanding
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}|_{\mathrm{pars}(\Phi_{i})}
.
Let
\mathcal{J}
be a pre-fixed
\mathcal{H}_{\omega}
-point of
\mathcal{C}_{\Phi_{i}}
expanding
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}|_{\mathrm{pars}(\Phi_{i})}
.
We will show that:

(i)

[\bar{t}]\in P^{\mathcal{J}}
if
P(\bar{t})\in\Gamma_{\omega}
for all
P\in\mathrm{def}(\Phi_{i})
;

(ii)

\mathcal{C}_{\Phi_{i}}(\mathcal{J})\sqsubseteq\mathcal{J}
.

Since, by definition,
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}|_{\mathrm{sym}(\Phi_{i})}
is the least
\mathrm{sym}(\Phi_{i})
-structure expanding
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}|_{\mathrm{pars}(\Phi_{i})}
that satisfies these properties, this implies that
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}|_{\mathrm{sym}(\Phi_{i})}\sqsubseteq\mathcal{J}
, showing that
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}|_{\mathrm{sym}(\Phi_{i})}
is indeed the least pre-fixed
\mathcal{H}_{\omega}
-point of
\mathcal{C}_{\Phi_{i}}
expanding
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}|_{\mathrm{pars}(\Phi_{i})}
.
Note that
(ii)
is immediately satisfied, as
\mathcal{J}
is a pre-fixed
\mathcal{H}_{\omega}
-point of
\mathcal{C}_{\Phi_{i}}
.
It thus remains to show
(i)
.

Since
\mathcal{J}
is an
\mathcal{H}_{\omega}
-point, there exists, for every
P\in\mathrm{def}(\Phi_{i})
, an FO
\xspace
-formula
F_{P}
and an
\mathsf{ar}(P)
-tuple
\bar{z}_{P}
of distinct object symbols such that

(25)

P^{\mathcal{J}}=\{[\bar{t}]\in\mathsf{dom}(\mathcal{J})^{\mathsf{ar}(P)}\mid\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\models F_{P}[\bar{t}/\bar{z}_{P}]\}.

Suppose that
P(\bar{t})\in\Gamma_{\omega}
.
Then
P(\bar{t})\in\Gamma_{j}
for some
j\in\mathbb{N}
.
Consider the schedule element
\langle P(\bar{t}),F_{\Pi}\rangle
, where
\Pi=\mathrm{def}(\Phi_{i})
.
It occurs infinitely often in the schedule
(e_{i})_{i}
.
By construction of
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\Delta_{\omega}
, the sequent
\Gamma_{j+1},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\Delta_{j+1}
is one of the premises of the derivation

(def L)

minor premises
\Gamma_{i},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n},F_{P}[\bar{t}]\vdash\Delta_{i}
\Gamma_{i},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\Delta_{i}

where the minor premises
\Gamma_{i},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n},\psi[F_{\Pi}/\Pi]\vdash F_{Q}[\bar{s}],\Delta_{i}
correspond to the definitional rules
\forall\bar{y}:Q(\bar{s})\leftarrow\psi
in
\Phi_{i}
.
We claim that
\Gamma_{j+1},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\Delta_{j+1}
is the major premise
\Gamma_{j},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n},F_{P}[\bar{t}]\vdash\Delta_{j}
.
This would mean that
F_{P}[\bar{t}]\in\Gamma_{\omega}
, which would imply that
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\models F_{P}[\bar{t}]
by Lemma
D.7
, and that
[\bar{t}]\in P^{\mathcal{J}}
by (
25
), thus proving
(i)
.

We show the claim by contradiction.
Suppose that
\Gamma_{j+1},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\Delta_{j+1}
were one of the minor premises.
Then it would be of the form
\Gamma_{j},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\psi[F_{\Pi}/\Pi]\vdash F_{Q}[\bar{s}],\Delta_{j}
for
a definitional rule
\forall\bar{y}:Q(\bar{s})\leftarrow\psi
in
\Phi_{i}
.
Then
\psi[F_{\Pi}/\Pi]\in\Gamma_{\omega}
and
F_{Q}[\bar{s}]\in\Delta_{\omega}
.
By Lemma
D.7
, the latter entails that
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\not\models F_{Q}[\bar{s}]
, and by (
25
),
[\bar{s}]\not\in Q^{\mathcal{J}}
.
We now show that
\mathcal{J}\models\psi
.
It now suffices to show that
\mathcal{J}\models\psi
.
Indeed, by
(ii)
,
\mathcal{J}\models\psi
implies that
[\bar{s}]\in Q^{\mathcal{J}}
, yielding the desired contradiction.
We prove that
\mathcal{J}\models\psi
by structural induction on
\psi
, using the fact that
\psi[F_{\Pi}/\Pi]\in\Gamma_{\omega}
.

Case
:
\psi
is of the form
t=s
.
Then
(t=s)\doteq\psi[F_{\Pi}/\Pi]\in\Gamma_{\omega}
.
By rule
(i)
of Definition
D.2
, this means that
t\sim s
.
Thus,
\mathcal{J}\models t=s
.

Case
:
\psi
is of the form
R(\bar{v})
.

Subcase
:
R
is defined by
\Phi_{i}
.
Then
F_{R}[\bar{v}]\doteq\psi[F_{\Pi}/\Pi]\in\Gamma_{\omega}
.
By Lemma
D.7
,
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\models F_{R}[\bar{v}]
.
By (
25
),
[\bar{v}]\in R^{\mathcal{J}}
.
Therefore,
\mathcal{J}\models R(\bar{v})
.

Subcase
:
R
is not defined by
\Phi_{i}
.
Then
R(\bar{v})\doteq\psi[F_{\Pi}/\Pi]\in\Gamma_{\omega}
.
By Lemma
D.7
,
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\models R(\bar{v})
, and since
\mathcal{J}
and
\mathcal{I}_{\omega}
differ only on
\mathrm{def}(\Phi_{i})
,
\mathcal{J}\models R(\bar{v})
.

Case
:
\psi
is of the form
\psi_{1}\land\psi_{2}
.
Then
\psi_{1}[F_{\Pi}/\Pi]\land\psi_{2}[F_{\Pi}/\Pi]\doteq\psi[F_{\Pi}/\Pi]\in\Gamma_{\omega}
.
By construction of
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\Delta_{\omega}
, the set
\Gamma_{\omega}
contains
\psi_{1}[F_{\Pi}/\Pi]
and
\psi_{2}[F_{\Pi}/\Pi]
.
By (the last) induction hypothesis,
\mathcal{J}\models\psi_{1}
and
\mathcal{J}\models\psi_{1}
.
Hence,
\mathcal{J}\models\psi_{1}\land\psi_{2}
.

Case
:
\psi
is of the form
\psi_{1}\lor\psi_{2}
.
Then
\psi_{1}[F_{\Pi}/\Pi]\lor\psi_{2}[F_{\Pi}/\Pi]\doteq\psi[F_{\Pi}/\Pi]\in\Gamma_{\omega}
.
By construction of
\Gamma_{\omega},\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\Delta_{\omega}
the set
\Gamma_{\omega}
contains
\psi_{1}[F_{\Pi}/\Pi]
or
\psi_{2}[F_{\Pi}/\Pi]
.
By (the last) induction hypothesis,
\mathcal{J}\models\psi_{1}
or
\mathcal{J}\models\psi_{1}
.
Hence,
\mathcal{J}\models\psi_{1}\lor\psi_{2}
.
∎

In conclusion, we have constructed a Henkin structure
(\mathcal{I}_{\omega},\mathcal{H}_{\omega})
(Lemma
D.9
) such that
(\mathcal{I}_{\omega},\mathcal{H}_{\omega})\models_{\mathrm{H}}\Phi_{i}
for all
i\in\{1,\dots,n\}
(Lemma
D.10
),
(\mathcal{I}_{\omega},\mathcal{H}_{\omega})\models_{\mathrm{H}}\varphi
for all
\varphi\in\Gamma_{\omega}
and
(\mathcal{I}_{\omega},\mathcal{H}_{\omega})\not\models_{\mathrm{H}}\varphi
for all
\varphi\in\Delta_{\omega}
(Lemma
D.7
).
Since
\Gamma\subseteq\Gamma_{\omega}
and
\Delta\subseteq\Delta_{\omega}
,
(\mathcal{I}_{\omega},\mathcal{H}_{\omega})
is a counter-model of the original sequent
\Gamma,\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\Delta
.
This finishes the proof of Theorem
8.5
.

D.2.
Proof of Lemma
8.8

We prove Lemma
8.8
:

See
8.8

We do so by first proving a few auxiliary lemmas:

Lemma D.11.

Let
\Gamma,\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\Delta
be a regular sequent.
Let
R
and
S
be two predicate symbols with the same arity, such that
R
is not defined by any definition
\Phi_{i}
.
Let
\Gamma^{\mathrm{s}},\Phi_{1}^{\mathrm{s}},\dots,\Phi_{n}^{\mathrm{s}}\vdash\Delta^{\mathrm{s}}
denote the sequent obtained from
\Gamma,\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\Delta
by replacing every atom of the form
R(\bar{t})
by
\lnot S(\bar{t})
.
If
\Gamma,\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\Delta
is cut-free provable, then so is
\Gamma^{\mathrm{s}},\Phi_{1}^{\mathrm{s}},\dots,\Phi_{n}^{\mathrm{s}}\vdash\Delta^{\mathrm{s}}
.

Proof.

Suppose that
\Gamma,\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\Delta
admits a cut-free proof
T
.
We show by induction on the height of
T
that
\Gamma^{\mathrm{s}},\Phi_{1}^{\mathrm{s}},\dots,\Phi_{n}^{\mathrm{s}}\vdash\Delta^{\mathrm{s}}
is cut-free provable.
The most interesting cases are those corresponding to the rules (def R) and (def L).
As an illustration, we also discuss the cases corresponding to (ax), (
=
R) and (
\lnot
L).

Case
:
\Gamma,\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\Delta
is derived by (ax).
Then
\bot\in\Gamma
,
\top\in\Delta
, or there exists a formula
\varphi
that is both in
\Gamma
and in
\Delta
.
In the latter case, the formula
\varphi^{\mathrm{s}}
, obtained from
\varphi
by replacing every atom of the form
R(\bar{t})
by
\lnot S(\bar{t})
, is both in
\Gamma^{\mathrm{s}}
and
\Delta^{\mathrm{s}}
.
In any case,
\Gamma^{\mathrm{s}},\Phi_{1}^{\mathrm{s}},\dots,\Phi_{n}^{\mathrm{s}}\vdash\Delta^{\mathrm{s}}
is derivable by (ax).

Case
:
\Gamma,\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\Delta
is derived by (
=
R).
Then
\Delta
contains a formula of the form
t=t
.
Then so does
\Delta^{\mathrm{s}}
.
Hence,
\Gamma^{\mathrm{s}},\Phi_{1}^{\mathrm{s}},\dots,\Phi_{n}^{\mathrm{s}}\vdash\Delta^{\mathrm{s}}
is derivable by (
=
R).

Case
:
\Gamma,\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\Delta
is derived by (
\lnot

Then the final step of

is of the form

(
\lnot

\Gamma^{\prime},{\Phi_{1}},\dots,{\Phi_{n}}\vdash\varphi,\Delta
\Gamma^{\prime},{\Phi_{1}},\dots,{\Phi_{n}},\lnot\varphi\vdash\Delta

where

.
By induction hypothesis,
\Gamma^{\prime\mathrm{s}},\Phi_{1}^{\mathrm{s}},\dots,\Phi_{n}^{\mathrm{s}}\vdash\varphi^{\mathrm{s}},\Delta^{\mathrm{s}}
is cut-free provable.
By application of (

\Gamma^{\prime\mathrm{s}},\Phi_{1}^{\mathrm{s}},\dots,\Phi_{n}^{\mathrm{s}},\neg\varphi^{\mathrm{s}}\vdash\Delta^{\mathrm{s}}
.
Since
\Gamma^{\prime\mathrm{s}}\cup\{\neg\varphi^{\mathrm{s}}\}=\Gamma^{\mathrm{s}}
, this means that
\Gamma^{\mathrm{s}},\Phi_{1}^{\mathrm{s}},\dots,\Phi_{n}^{\mathrm{s}}\vdash\Delta^{\mathrm{s}}
is cut-free provable.

Case
:
\Gamma,\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\Delta
is derived by (def R).
Then the final step of
T
is of the form

(def R)

\Gamma,{\Phi_{1}},\dots,{\Phi_{n}}\vdash\varphi[\bar{s}/\bar{x}],\Delta^{\prime}
\Gamma,{\Phi_{1}},\dots,{\Phi_{n}}\vdash P(\bar{t}[\bar{s}/\bar{x}]),\Delta^{\prime}

for a definitional rule
\forall\bar{x}:P(\bar{t})\leftarrow\varphi
in a definition
{\Phi_{i}}
and a tuple of terms
\bar{s}
with the same length as
\bar{x}
.
Here
\Delta^{\prime}\coloneq\Delta\setminus\{P(\bar{t}[\bar{s}/\bar{x}])\}
.
By induction hypothesis,
\Gamma^{\mathrm{s}},\Phi_{1}^{\mathrm{s}},\dots,\Phi_{n}^{\mathrm{s}}\vdash\varphi[\bar{s}/\bar{x}]^{\mathrm{s}},\Delta^{\prime\mathrm{s}}
is cut-free provable.
Note that
\forall\bar{x}:P(\bar{t})\leftarrow\varphi^{\mathrm{s}}
is a definitional rule in
\Phi_{i}^{\mathrm{s}}
, and that
\varphi[\bar{s}/\bar{x}]^{\mathrm{s}}\doteq\varphi^{\mathrm{s}}[\bar{s}/\bar{x}]
.
By application of (def R), the sequent
\Gamma^{\mathrm{s}},\Phi_{1}^{\mathrm{s}},\dots,\Phi_{n}^{\mathrm{s}}\vdash P(\bar{t}[\bar{s}/\bar{x}]),\Delta^{\prime\mathrm{s}}
is cut-free provable.
Since
\Delta^{\prime\mathrm{s}}\cup\{P(\bar{t}[\bar{s}/\bar{x}])\}=\Delta^{\mathrm{s}}
,
30
30

30

Here we use the fact that
P
is distinct from

, as

is not defined by any definition

, whence

this means that

is cut-free provable.

Case
:
\Gamma,\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}\vdash\Delta
is derived by (def L).
Then the final step of

is of the form

(def L)

minor premises

for a defined predicate

, the sequent

is cut-free provable.
Here we used the fact that

with

, as

is not defined by any definition
\Phi_{i}
.
Since
\Gamma^{\prime\mathrm{s}}\cup\{P(\bar{v})\}=\Gamma^{\mathrm{s}}
, this means that
\Gamma^{\mathrm{s}},\Phi_{1}^{\mathrm{s}},\dots,\Phi_{n}^{\mathrm{s}}\vdash\Delta^{\mathrm{s}}
is cut-free provable.

Lemma D.12.

Let
\Gamma
and
\Delta
be sets of
FO(ID)
\xspace
-formulas and
\varphi
and
FO(ID)
\xspace
-formula.
If
\Gamma\vdash\lnot\varphi,\Delta
is cut-free provable, then so is
\Gamma,\varphi\vdash\Delta
.

Proof.

This is shown by induction on the height of a cut-free proof
T
of
\Gamma\vdash\lnot\varphi,\Delta
.
We discuss the cases corresponding to the rules (ax) and (
\lnot
R).
The other cases are straightforward.

Case
:
\Gamma\vdash\lnot\varphi,\Delta
is derived by (ax).
If
\bot\in\Gamma
,
\top\in\Delta
, or
\Gamma\cap\Delta\neq\emptyset
, then
\Gamma,\varphi\vdash\Delta
is also derivable by (ax).
Otherwise,
\lnot\varphi\in\Gamma
.
In that case,
\Gamma,\varphi\vdash\Delta
is derivable by (ax) and (
\lnot
L).

Case
:
\Gamma\vdash\lnot\varphi,\Delta
is derived by (
\lnot
R).

Subcase
: the last inference in
T
is of the form

(
\lnot
R)

\Gamma,\varphi\vdash\Delta
\Gamma\vdash\lnot\varphi,\Delta

Then the subtree of
T
with root labeled by
\Gamma,\varphi\vdash\Delta
is a cut-free proof of
\Gamma,\varphi\vdash\Delta
.

Subcase
: the last inference in
T
is of the form

(
\lnot
R)

\Gamma,\psi\vdash\lnot\varphi,\Delta^{\prime}
\Gamma\vdash\lnot\psi,\lnot\varphi,\Delta^{\prime}

where
\Delta^{\prime}\coloneq\Delta\setminus\{\lnot\psi\}
.
By induction hypothesis,
\Gamma,\psi,\varphi\vdash\Delta^{\prime}
is cut-free provable.
By application of (
\lnot
R), so is
\Gamma,\varphi\vdash\lnot\psi,\Delta^{\prime}
.
∎

Lemma D.13.

Let
\Gamma
and
\Delta
be sets of
FO(ID)
\xspace
-formulas.
Let
Q
be a
k
-ary predicate symbol,
i\in\{0,\dots,k\}
,
\bar{t}
an
i
-tuple of terms, and
\bar{y}
a
(k-i)
-tuple of object symbols.
If
\Gamma,\forall\bar{y}:\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})\Leftrightarrow\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})\vdash\Delta
is cut-free provable, then so is
\Gamma,\forall\bar{y}:Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})\lor\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})\vdash\Delta
.
31
31

31

Writing out
\bar{t}=(t_{1},\dots,t_{i})
and
\bar{y}=(y_{1},\dots,y_{k-i})
,
Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})
stands for
Q(t_{1},\dots,t_{i},y_{1},\dots,y_{k-i})
.

Proof.

We show the statement by induction on the height of a cut-free proof
T
of a sequent of the form
\Gamma,\forall\bar{y}:\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})\Leftrightarrow\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})\vdash\Delta
.
We discuss the cases corresponding to the inference rules (ax), (
\Leftrightarrow
L), (
\forall
L).
The other cases are straightforward.

Case
:
\Gamma,\forall\bar{y}:\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})\Leftrightarrow\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})\vdash\Delta
is derived by (ax).
If
\bot\in\Gamma
,
\top\in\Delta
, or
\Gamma\cap\Delta\neq\emptyset
, then
\Gamma,\forall\bar{y}:Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})\lor\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})\vdash\Delta
is also derivable by (ax).
Otherwise,
\forall\bar{y}:\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})\Leftrightarrow\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})\in\Delta
.
It is straightforward to show that
\vdash\forall\bar{y}:\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})\Leftrightarrow\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})
is cut-free provable.
Hence, by application of (wk), so is
\Gamma,\forall\bar{y}:Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})\lor\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})\vdash\Delta
in the latter case.

Case
:
\Gamma,\forall\bar{y}:\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})\Leftrightarrow\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})\vdash\Delta
is derived by (
\Leftrightarrow
L).

Subcase
: the last inference in
T
is of the form

(
\Leftrightarrow
L)

\Gamma,\lnot Q(\bar{t})\vdash\Delta
\Gamma\vdash\lnot Q(\bar{t}),\Delta
\Gamma,\lnot Q(\bar{t})\Leftrightarrow\lnot Q(\bar{t}),\vdash\Delta

(This is possible only if
i=0
.)
Then
\Gamma,\lnot Q(\bar{t})\vdash\Delta
and
\Gamma\vdash\lnot Q(\bar{t}),\Delta
are cut-free provable.
By Lemma
D.12
,
\Gamma,Q(\bar{t})\vdash\Delta
is cut-free provable as well, and by application of (
\lor
L), so is
\Gamma,Q(\bar{t})\lor\lnot Q(\bar{t}),\vdash\Delta
.

Subcase
: the last inference in
T
is of the form

(
\Leftrightarrow
L)

\Gamma^{\prime},\forall\bar{y}:\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})\Leftrightarrow\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y}),\varphi,\psi\vdash\Delta
\Gamma^{\prime},\forall\bar{y}:\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})\Leftrightarrow\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})\vdash\varphi,\psi,\Delta
\Gamma^{\prime},\forall\bar{y}:\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})\Leftrightarrow\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y}),\varphi\Leftrightarrow\psi\vdash\Delta

where
\Gamma^{\prime}\coloneq\Gamma\setminus\{\varphi\Leftrightarrow\psi\}
.
By induction hypothesis, the sequents
\Gamma^{\prime},\forall\bar{y}:Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})\lor\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y}),\varphi,\psi\vdash\Delta
and
\Gamma^{\prime},\forall\bar{y}:Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})\lor\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y}),\varphi,\psi\vdash\Delta
are cut-free provable.
Then, by application of (
\Leftrightarrow
L), so is
\Gamma^{\prime},\forall\bar{y}:Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})\lor\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y}),\varphi\Leftrightarrow\psi\vdash\Delta
.

Case
:
\Gamma,\forall\bar{y}:\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})\Leftrightarrow\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})\vdash\Delta
is derived by (
\forall
L).

Subcase
: the last inference in
T
is of the form

(
\forall
L)

\Gamma,\forall\bar{y}^{\prime}:\lnot Q(\bar{t},t,\bar{y}^{\prime})\Leftrightarrow\lnot Q(\bar{t},t,\bar{y}^{\prime})\vdash\Delta
\Gamma,\forall\bar{y}:\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})\Leftrightarrow\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})\vdash\Delta

where, writing out
\bar{y}=(y_{1},y_{2},\dots,y_{i})
,
\bar{y}^{\prime}
denotes the tuple
(y_{2},\dots,y_{i})
.
By induction hypothesis,
\Gamma,\forall\bar{y}^{\prime}:Q(\bar{t},t,\bar{y}^{\prime})\lor\lnot Q(\bar{t},t,\bar{y}^{\prime})\vdash\Delta
is cut-free provable, and by application of (
\forall
L), so is
\Gamma,\forall\bar{y}:Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})\lor\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})\vdash\Delta
.

Subcase
: the last inference in
T
is of the form

(
\forall
L)

\Gamma^{\prime},\forall\bar{y}:\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})\Leftrightarrow\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y}),\varphi[t/x]\vdash\Delta
\Gamma^{\prime},\forall\bar{y}:\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})\Leftrightarrow\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y}),\forall x:\varphi\vdash\Delta

where
\Gamma^{\prime}\coloneq\Gamma\setminus\{\forall x:\varphi\}
.
By induction hypothesis,
\Gamma^{\prime},\forall\bar{y}:Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})\lor\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y}),\varphi[t/x]\vdash\Delta
is cut-free provable, and by application of (
\forall
L), so is
\Gamma^{\prime},\forall\bar{y}:Q(\bar{t},\bar{y})\lor\lnot Q(\bar{t},\bar{y}),\forall x:\varphi\vdash\Delta
.
∎

We are now ready to prove Lemma
8.8
:

Proof of Lemma
8.8
.

Suppose that
\Gamma,\Omega,\Phi_{1}^{\mathrm{c}},\dots,\Phi_{n}^{\mathrm{c}}\vdash\Delta
is cut-free provable.
Then, by repeated application of Lemma
D.11
(with
R=Q^{\mathrm{c}}
and
S=Q
for each predicate symbol
Q
appearing negatively in the body of a rule of a definition
\Phi_{i}
), so is
\Gamma,\Omega^{\prime},{\Phi_{1}},\dots,{\Phi_{n}}\vdash\Delta
, where
\Omega^{\prime}
consists of all formulas of the form
\forall\bar{y}:\lnot Q(\bar{y})\Leftrightarrow\lnot Q(\bar{y})
such that
Q
appears negatively in the body of a rule of a definition
\Phi_{i}
.
Next, by repeated application of Lemma
D.13
(with
i=0
),
\Gamma,\Omega^{\prime\prime},{\Phi_{1}},\dots,{\Phi_{n}}\vdash\Delta
is cut-free provable, where
\Omega^{\prime\prime}
consists of all formulas of the form
\forall\bar{y}:Q(\bar{y})\lor\lnot Q(\bar{y})
such that
Q
appears negatively in the body of a rule of a definition
\Phi_{i}
.
It is straightforward to check that
\vdash\forall\bar{y}:Q(\bar{y})\lor\lnot Q(\bar{y})
is cut-free provable for every such formula.
By repeated application of (wk) and (cut) on these formulas, we deduce that
\Gamma,{\Phi_{1}},\dots,{\Phi_{n}}\vdash\Delta
is provable with elementary cuts.
∎
